## Document 064: 2026年10月财政数据快评：离岸信托补税带动个税高增长

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月财政数据快评：离岸信托补税带动个税高增长.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
1-10月，全国一般公共预算收入约 19.6万亿元，同比增长 4.9%。全国一般公共预算支出约 22.8万亿元，
同比增长 1.0%。
评论：
10 月财政收入回升，广义支出转正，政策力度指数继续回升。
第一本账收入转正，支出小幅增长。10月收入转正，税收增速回升主要受一次性补税推动，剔除后税收收
入基本持平；支出仅小幅增长，后续有望向预算增速追赶。
第二本账收支双双转正。10月第二本账收入与支出均由负转正，土地出让收入降幅收窄；低基数与土地以
外的其他支出加快共同推动改善，后续仍需观察地产走势与债务资金拨付情况。
总体来看，广义财政收支双双转正，力度指数连续第三个月回升。收入改善包含个税一次性补税和低基数
影响，广义财政缺口同比收缩幅度略有扩大，财政实际扩张仍偏温和，后续支出端仍有修复空间。
风险提示：海外市场动荡，存在不确定性。

### Page 2

 一般公共预算：收入转正、支出小幅增长
10 月财政收入转正，但一次性补税贡献较大。1-10月财政收入累计同比 4.9%，前值（1-9月）5.1%，延
续回落。其中税收收入累计同比 6.0%，前值 6.1%。单月来看，一般公共预算收入当月同比 3.4%，前值-0.1%，
由负转正；税收收入当月同比 5.9%，前值 0.2%；非税收入当月同比-23.6%，前值-1.0%，降幅明显扩大。
税收回升主要由个税一次性补税贡献，非税收入大幅下降则形成拖累；剔除该项补税后，税收收入当月同
比约 0.1%，当月收入同比约-1.9%，收入端继续回落。
图1：一般公共预算收入累计与当月增速 图2：一般公共收入月度进度
资料来源:WIND，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:WIND，国信证券经济研究所整理
图3：税收收入与非税收入增速 图4：各税种同比增速与前值对比
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
分税种看，个税一次性补税驱动高增长，增值税小幅转负。主要税种中个人所得税同比 89.5%，较前值（10.5%）

大幅上行，拉动税收收入 6.8 个百分点、为最大拉动项，主要是离岸信托补税入库 1200 亿元的一次性因
素，剔除后个税同比 12.8%，处于合理区间。国内增值税同比-0.1%，较前值（7.3%）转负；企业所得税同
比-0.1%，基本持平；消费税同比 4.1%，较前值（-8.5%）转正，拉动税收 0.3个百分点；进口环节增值税
和消费税同比 17.8%，拉动税收 1.3个百分点，与进口的高增长一致。房地产相关税种整体同比-5.7%，降
幅较前值（-14.6%）收窄，其中契税（-20.4%）和土地增值税（-39.9%）仍然疲弱，对应 10 月降幅扩大
的房地产投资数据。此外，证券交易印花税同比-33.7%，降幅较前值（-11.5%）扩大，拖累税收 0.3个百
分点，主要是成交量逐渐走低。

### Page 3

图5：各税种对税收收入的拉动排序 图6：主要税种同比增速
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
支出端小幅增长。一般公共预算支出当月同比 0.5%，较前值（0.03%）小幅回升。节奏上，一般公共预算
支出当月完成 5.9%，比去年同期（6.0%）略慢。累计来看，1-10月第一本账支出同比 1.0%，与前值持平；
累计进度 76.0%，与去年同期（76.0%）大体相当，但离 4.4%的预算支出增速仍有较大差距。后续有待存
量资金加快拨付带动支出回升，全年增速预计回升至 1.5%左右。
结构上，民生支出转正，基建支出降幅扩大。民生类支出同比 3.2%，较前值（-0.9%）转正。其中，教育
支出同比 4.6%，前值 3.5%；社保就业支出同比-0.1%，降幅较前值（-4.8%）明显收窄；卫生健康支出同
比 9.4%，较前值（-2.9%）转正；文化娱乐体育传媒支出同比-5.3%，前值 1.6%，再度转负。基建类支出
同比-1.7%，前值-1.3%，拖累支出 0.4 个百分点。其中交通运输支出同比-18.4%，前值-10.8%，降幅扩大；

城乡社区事务支出同比-2.0%，较前值（7.9%）转负；农林水事务支出同比 6.4%，较前值（-0.2%）转正；
节能环保支出同比-2.3%，降幅较前值（-13.7%）继续收窄。此外，债务付息支出同比-4.1%，降幅较前值
（-1.2%）扩大；科学技术支出同比-22.9%，较前值（6.9%）转负，拖累支出 1.0 个百分点、为最大单项
拖累。

 政府性基金预算：收支双双转正
10 月政府性基金收入同比 9.5%（前值-15.9%），支出同比 17.2%（前值-12.3%），均由负转正。土地出让
收入同比-9.0%，降幅较前值（-23.2%）收窄；其他收入同比 55.6%，较前值（2.3%）明显加快。支出端主
要是除土地出让收入相关支出外的其他支出同比由-8.4%升至 28.9%，反映债务资金拨付有所加快。同时，
2025年 10月政府性基金收入、支出分别同比-18.4%、-38.2%，低基数也是本月双双转正的重要原因。
1-10月，第二本账收入累计同比-15.5%，支出累计同比-13.8%。其中土地出让收入 18526 亿元，同比-25.8%，

降幅较 1-9月（-27.9%）收窄，但仍处深度负增长区间，按当前降幅外推，全年约 3.1万亿元。后续来看，
低基数可能继续支撑第二本账收支同比读数，但土地出让仍然偏弱，修复持续性主要取决于存量债务资金
落地的情况。

 总结：广义收支双双转正，力度指数继续回升
财政力度指数连续第三个月回升，但发力迹象并不明显。一方面，广义收支双双转正。10月广义支出同比
4.7%，较前值-3.7%转正；广义收入同比 4.3%，前值-3.5%，同样转正。1-10 月，广义财政支出累计同比
-2.9%，完成度 71.1%，低于去年同期（72.7%）；广义财政收入累计同比 1.7%，完成度 80.6%。另一方面，
赤字端同比收缩幅度略有扩大。1-10月累计的广义财政缺口为 7.3 万亿元，较 2025年同期（8.6万亿元）
减少约 1.3万亿元，同比-14.9%，收缩幅度较 1-9月（-14.4%）略有扩大。
后续来看，财政支出仍有提速空间，收入端则需关注一次性因素消退后的表现。10月收支改善有个税补税
和低基数支撑。随着 5500 亿元债务限额下达、8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具持续投放，叠加专项债和超

长期特别国债存量资金加快使用，广义财政支出有望延续修复，关注资金拨付与实物工作量形成情况。此
外，10 月财政存款余额 9.4万亿元，较多资金仍未落地，或形成万亿规模的结转结余资金留到明年上半年。
图15：广义财政支出同比增速 图16：财政力度指数拆解

## Document 125: 261112_ms_accton

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_ms_accton.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q26 GM slightly lower: Off revenue of NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY),
GM contracted by 2.1ppt QoQ to 17.6% (vs. MSe and consensus both at 18.5%) on
higher component costs. With opex of NT$5,162mn (opex ratio 4.3% vs. MSe 4.3%/
consensus  4.0%), OP was NT$16,122mn (OPM 13.4%), 6%/5% lower than MSe of NT
$17,183mn (OPM 14.2%)/consensus  of NT$17,047mn (OPM 14.4%), respectively. With
a narrower non-op gain of NT$94mn, net profit came in at NT$13,074mn (EPS NT

$23.4), 2%/5% short of MSe/consensus of NT$13,328mn (EPS NT$23.9)/NT
$13,748mn (EPS NT$24.5).
Outlook still on optimistic side: We expect Accton to grow revenue sequentially
into 4Q26. Network switch customers will likely continue to pull in more for their AI
clusters amid 800G migration. Meanwhile, its AI accelerator customer should ramp
more of its new generation product and increase adoption of liquid cooling thermal
solutions. To mitigate pressure from elevated component costs, it has negotiated

with customers for them to consign some components, e.g., DRAM, from late 3Q26.
Although this might cap revenue growth upside for Accton, it should help on the
GM side. We model revenue of NT$130,083mn (+8% QoQ/+81% YoY), gross margin
of 20.0% (+2.4ppt QoQ), operating margin of 15.5% (+2.1ppt QoQ)  and EPS of NT
$28.2 (+21% QoQ/+88% YoY). Looking to 2027, we see revenue growth of NT
$526,019mn (+26% YoY) and GM of 20.0%, for EPS NT$112.9 EPS (+31% YoY).

Stay OW: We believe Accton should continue to benefit from networking and
compute demand growth amid AI deployment, reflecting its system integration
capability, customer relationships, and execution. For the longer term,  it will likely
tap into opportunities in rack level solutions, optical-based switches, etc. We lower
our PT to NT$2,250 (20x 2027 P/E) from NT$3,500 (30x 2027 P/E), mostly owing
to more conservative sentiment toward AI names.

catalyst for the share price in the coming months.

6
Valuation Methodology and Risks
OW with PT of NT$2,250 (previously NT$3,500)
Price target (base case scenario value) –  NT$2,250 (-36%): Our new price target is based
on 20x our 2027 EPS estimate (30x previously). We lower our target multiple to factor in
recent market sentiment toward AI names. We see our target multiple supported by the
45% operating profit CAGR we project for 2025-28. This implies a PEG of 0.4 vs. its
average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for an operating

profit CAGR of 32%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,350 (-20%): We apply 30x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (36x previously). This scenario reflects stronger demand for network switches in
both data center and enterprise applications as well as more orders for AI accelerator
modules.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,800 (-39%): We apply 10x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (16x previously). This scenario reflects lukewarm data center and enterprise

capex and continued dominance by merchant GPUs in AI servers.
Downside risks to our positive thesis
• Weaker data center capex
• Slower network switch spec migration
• InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
• Softer custom AI accelerator growth
• Softer ODM direct business model adoptions

network switch suppliers in the ODM direct
market, will continue to gain from increasing
data trafﬁc (22% CAGR seen for 2023-30)

processing throughput.

customer base.

contribution from another hyperscaler.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

貿聯-KY (3665 TT) 
併購綜效超預期，獲利動能再加速

永豐觀點 
併購 Interplex、新富生貢獻雙雙超預期，獲利上修循環啟動。 
 投資評價與建議 
重申買進建議：研究部本次上修貿聯財務預估：(1) Interplex 資料通訊業務
(Datacom Business)營收規模大幅超標：10 月首個完整併表月營收約新台幣 21
億元，大幅優於研究部原先預期之約 13 億元，研究部同步上修其 2026~2028 年
營收與獲利貢獻預估。(2) 新富生月營收規模自年初以來已成長逾一倍，憑藉資料
中心高階光纖跳線的製造與品質優勢，持續拓展新客戶，研究部預估其 2027 年營
收可持續倍增。(3) 產品組合轉佳、營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 毛利率季增 1.8 個
百點至 32.53%，且營業費用率降至約 12%，帶動營益率創 20.78%新高。綜合預
估貿聯 2026-2028 年集團營收分別為 1,074、1,659、2,161 億元(+51%、+55%、
+30%YoY)；稅後淨利 150、267、356 元(+66%、+79%、+33%YoY)，完全稀
釋後 EPS 分別為 75.25、134.07 及 178.24 元。考量 AI 供應鏈遇短期逆風，影響
評價表現，研究部將本益比調整為 18 倍，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027
年及 2028 年之平均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

ESG 評析 
貿聯企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

### Page 2

2

貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：
貿聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、
工業用 31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設
備(SPE)占 13％、其他事業占 41％。貿聯主要核心能力，在於高技術及客製化開
發零組件製造，產品開發以多角化及差異化策略，2026/1 完成收購新富生光電
(XFS)，2026/9/23 完成收購 Interplex 集團資料通訊業務(Interplex Datacom)，
產品線由電源與數據連接延伸至光纖跳線、機殼及機櫃等精密機構件，朝 AI 機櫃
次系統解決方案供應商發展。 
3Q26 財報公布，優於市場預期，營收與獲利同創新高，營益率首度突破 20%：
3Q26 營收 278.75 億元(+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY)，毛利率 32.53%，季增 1.8
個百分點；營業利益率 20.78%，季增 3.6 個百分點、年增 1.2 個百分點，為公司
首度突破 20%，獲利表現優於市場預期。成長主要來自 HPC，3Q26 HPC 營收季
增 30%、年增 61%，營收比重由 2Q26 的 42%回升至 46%，其中電源產品為成
長主力，帶動產品組合改善；半導體設備營收亦季增 11%、年增 70%，各事業全
面成長。費用方面，營收規模擴大但營業費用未等比例增加，營業費用率降至約
12%的歷史低點，顯示獲利率改善來自規模效益與費用效率，而非一次性因素，具
備延續性。稅後淨利 42.93 億元(+44.0% QoQ、+63.3% YoY)，稀釋後 EPS 21.51
元，營收、淨利與 EPS 皆創歷史新高；前三季累計 EPS 48.04 元，已超越 2025 全
年之 46.07 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

研究部上修 2026~2028 年營收獲利預估，原因有三： 
1. Interplex 資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)營收提前兩年達標：Interplex
的資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)自 9/23 起併表，3Q26 僅貢獻 7 天營
收；10 月為首個完整併表月，帶動貿聯 10 月營收達 118.46 億元，月增 26.9%、
年增 80.0%，其中 Interplex 貢獻 6,700 萬美元(約新台幣 21 億元)。對照公司
宣布併購時揭露之年營收 3.92 億美元(月平均約 3,300 萬美元)，Interplex 月
營收已倍增，大幅優於市場預期。研究部看好併購綜效將逐步顯現：Interplex
主要供應 AI 伺服器機殼、機櫃等精密機構件，研究部認為其營收較併購時大
幅成長，主因 AI 機櫃出貨量較年初明顯放大，且併入貿聯後，Interplex 可運
用集團的資金、客戶關係與製造資源擴大接單，雙方產品整合後可提供涵蓋配
電、數據連接、機構件與液冷的機櫃次系統方案，單一 AI 機櫃之可供應產值
明顯擴大。研究部本次上修 2027、2028 年 Interplex Datacom Business 為
貿聯帶來的營收預估，分別為 268、308 億元(原預估 183、250 億元)。 
2. 新富生光通訊規模快速放大，營收占比持續提升：新富生(XFS) 專精於光纖互
連產品的設計與製造，主要產品包括單芯／雙芯跳線、光纖尾纖、多芯光纖以
及 MTP/MPO 高密度連接器，月營收規模自 2026 年初以來成長逾一倍，獲
利能力同步改善，展現規模化高精密光學互連業務的效益。資料中心對光纖組
件的品質與一致性要求遠高於一般應用，新富生在高階光纖跳線的製造經驗與
品質優勢，加上貿聯集團的客戶網絡與全球產能布局，有助其持續拓展新客戶。
隨 AI 資料中心機櫃間光纖連接需求成長，研究部預估新富生 2027 年營收可
望再翻倍增長，且其獲利率改善亦有助提升貿聯整體獲利結構。 
3. 3Q26 營益率創新高，營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 營業費用率約 12%，較 1H26
的 13.7%明顯下降，顯示貿聯在承接更大規模、更複雜的客戶專案時，製造、
工程與行政職能的效率同步提升，費用無須等比例增加。4Q26 起 Interplex
完整併表，營收規模再擴大，研究部預期營益率改善幅度可望大於毛利率，稅
後淨利成長幅度將高於營收。 
HPC 電源成長最強，新平台升級為下一波動能：3Q26 HPC 營收年增 61%、季增
30%，前三季累計年增 77%。隨運算功耗提升，電源產品切入規格更高的新一代
平台；數據連接方面，貿聯同時布局銅纜與光纖，因應不同傳輸距離與頻寬需求。
貿聯於 10 月 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出適用於 OCP 及 NVIDIA MGX 機櫃
之 800V/400VDC 高功率電源方案，以及 1.6T 液冷交換器 OSFP 連接器、機櫃管
理控制與漏液偵測系統，產品範圍由電源線材延伸至配電、液冷與監控。研究部認
為，隨 Vera Rubin 平台放量及 HVDC 架構導入，單一機櫃的電源產品規格與產值
將持續提升，為 2027 年 HPC 成長主軸。 
次世代光通訊布局：貿聯 2026 年先後於 OFC 展示 Optical Shuffle、於 Computex
展示 CPO 交換器托盤方案；9 月於 ECOC 2026 展示與 ams OSRAM 合作之
microVCSEL 光學互連方案，採用 850nm 二維可定址 microVCSEL 陣列，以多通
道、低速率(wide-and-slow)架構降低功耗與延遲，貿聯負責光纖陣列組裝、光學
封裝、光學測試與量產製造；10 月於 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出 CPO、
800G/1.6T 及 XPO 光收發模組、光纖陣列(FAU)與多芯光纖連接等方案。公司表
示產業未來可能並行採用多種光學架構，貿聯以製造與整合能力參與各技術路線，
不需押注單一方案。 
半導體設備成長幅度為產業兩倍以上：3Q26 半導體設備(SPE)營收年增 70%、季
增 11%，營收占比由 3Q25 的 11%提升至 13%，前三季累計年增 48%。根據國
際半導體產業協會（SEMI）於 2026 年 7 月公布之預測，全球前段晶圓製造設備

### Page 4

4

(WFE)市場 2026 年預估年增 23.1%至 1,439 億美元，貿聯 SPE 成長幅度約為產業
的兩倍；自 2023 年以來貿聯之 SPE 業務成長即大幅超越整體 WFE 市場，超額成
長主因貿聯在客戶端的角色由供應線材零組件，擴大至高階組裝、模組與次系統製
造、測試及系統整合，應用以薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備為主，微影與檢測設備為輔；隨
設備製程日益複雜，客戶傾向將更多製造工作委外，以集中資源於核心技術，貿聯
單一設備平台之內含價值隨之提升。SEMI 預估 WFE 市場 2027 年及 2028 年將分
別成長 21.8%及 14.1%，2028 年規模可望突破 2,000 億美元，主要由先進邏輯、
HBM 等先進記憶體投資帶動；公司亦表示，許多客戶對設備投資的強勁預期延伸
至 2030 年以後，且客戶正主動協助貿聯擴充產能，以支援其自身長期成長。貿聯
在 SPE 領域的投資以擴充製造產能為主，Interplex 的精密機械加工與次系統整合
能力亦可與 SPE 業務互補，為 HPC 以外的第二成長引擎。 
隨伺服器架構升級驅動產值倍增，2027~2028 年獲利成長路徑清晰，重申買進： 
研究部認為，貿聯本次財報的關鍵不只是單季獲利優於預期，而是兩項併購同時優
於預期，帶動營收基期結構性墊高：10 月營收 118.46 億元，較 3Q26 月平均約
93 億元高出約 27%。在 AI 機櫃同時需要電源、數據傳輸(銅纜與光纖)與精密機構
件的趨勢下，貿聯透過 Interplex 與新富生補齊產品版圖，單一機櫃可供應產值持
續擴大；而未來一年內，Interplex 完整併表(4Q26)、新富生營收比重提升(2027)、
Vera Rubin 平台與 HVDC 電 源 放 量 (2027~2028) 及 次 世 代 光 互 連 產 品
(2H27~2028)等成長催化劑將陸續到位，獲利上修動能明確。 
評價方面，短期因市場擔憂 GPU 產品推出節奏可能放緩，台灣 AI 供應鏈評價短
期受到壓抑。研究部認為，CSP 資本支出與現行平台訂單短期不受影響，且貿聯的
客戶與產品布局相對分散：公司採取平台中立策略，產品同時供應 GPU 與 ASIC
架構之 AI 機櫃，並服務多家雲端服務業者；銅纜與光纖、電源與機構件等產品線
亦不依賴單一技術路線；此外，半導體設備與其他事業合計仍占營收逾五成，營運
受單一平台影響的程度低於純 GPU 供應鏈。惟考量主要客戶經營層異動可能影響
新平台推出節奏，研究部於評價上給予折價，本益比由前次之 25 倍調整為 18 倍。
搭配本次上修後之 EPS，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027 年及 2028 年之平
均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Xuất hiện áp lực điều chỉnh sau khi xuyên thủng kênh hỗ trợ trung hạn, tuy nhiên 
xu hướng đi ngang chủ đạo chưa bị phá vỡ nên chỉ số được kỳ vọng xuất hiện các 
nhịp hồi phục tại vùng đáy ngắn/trung hạn

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -46.14 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Công nghệ, Bảo hiểm và Logistics là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,290 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và ACB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy trì bán ròng ở mức -864 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -10,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -22,338 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì bán ròng -842 hợp 
đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,945 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần điều chỉnh với 4 phiên giảm điểm liên tiếp, VN-Index đánh 
mất mốc tâm lý 1.700 điểm trong bối cảnh thanh khoản tiếp tục duy trì ở mức thấp và nhà 
đầu tư nước ngoài đẩy mạnh bán ròng, tập trung ở nhóm ngân hàng, Vingroup và FPT. 
Hiệu ứng tích cực từ các động thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản của NHNN qua nghiệp vụ mua vào 
ngoại tệ nhanh chóng hạ nhiệt, khi các phiên chào mua của NHNN đều diễn ra không 
thành công do tỷ giá bật tăng trở lại. Bên ngoài, TTCK toàn cầu tiếp tục chịu áp lực khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu AI biến động mạnh và kết quả kinh doanh tích cực của Nvidia không đủ 
xoa dịu lo ngại về đầu tư vào AI, cùng với kỳ vọng Fed nâng lãi suất trong tháng 12, gây 
thêm sức ép lên dòng vốn vào các thị trường mới nổi. Điểm tích cực là lực cầu bắt đáy 
xuất hiện trong phiên cuối tuần giúp chỉ số thu hẹp đà giảm và đóng cửa ở mức cao nhất 
phiên.

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Áp lực lợi nhuận trong chu kỳ mở rộng công suất 
19/11/2026

KQKD 3Q2026 suy giảm do khấu hao 
và biến động tỷ giá

Lượt khách ACV dự kiến tăng chậm 
hơn toàn ngành do tác động từ Phú 
Quốc và Gia Bình

Long Thành dự kiến khai thác từ cuối 
2026, tạo áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận 
giai đoạn đầu

Duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá 
mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu

3Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,066 tỷ VND (-6% yoy) và LNST công ty mẹ 
đạt 2,206 tỷ VND (-14% yoy). Lợi nhuận suy giảm chủ yếu do (1) khấu hao tăng từ 
các nhà ga mới và (2) ghi nhận lỗ tỷ giá 168 tỷ VND trong kỳ, trái ngược với khoản lãi 
tỷ giá cùng kỳ 2025. Lũy kế 9M2026, doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ lần lượt đạt 
19,226/7,435 tỷ VND (+0%/+2% yoy).

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa qua hệ thống ACV tăng trưởng bình 
quân 7%/5.5%/năm đến 2030, thấp hơn toàn ngành do (1) sân bay Phú Quốc 
không còn thuộc hệ thống ACV từ đầu 2026 và (2) một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội 
Bài dịch chuyển tự nhiên sang sân bay Gia Bình khi đi vào khai thác. Mức độ dịch 
chuyển trước năm 2030 dự kiến còn hạn chế do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ hoàn thiện 
sân bay và hạ tầng giao thông kết nối.

Cảng HKQT Long Thành giai đoạn 1 dự kiến khai thác một phần từ cuối tháng 12/2026 
và bắt đầu tiếp nhận các chuyến bay quốc tế đường dài từ Tân Sơn Nhất vào ngày 
28/03/2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV. Tuy nhiên, tốc độ 
chuyển dịch có thể chậm hơn kế hoạch do hạ tầng kết nối chưa hoàn thiện, trong khi 
chi phí khấu hao và vận hành tăng dự kiến gây áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận của ACV giai 
đoạn 2027–2028.

Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền, chúng tôi duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với ACV với giá mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng 
giá 4.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 18/11/2026.

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

(Dưới 3 tháng)

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành nến giảm điểm thân dài, thể hiện áp lực phân phối đang có 
phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, sau lần thứ 3 nỗ lực vượt cản 1.750 bất thành, chỉ số đã quay 
đầu lao dốc và xuyên thủng kênh hỗ trợ trung hạn, đồng thời phá vỡ cạnh dưới của một 
mẫu hình tam giác nhỏ, kết thúc nhịp hồi phục tích lũy ngắn hạn. Mặc dù những tín hiệu 
này đều mang tính tiêu cực, tuy nhiên khi đặt trong bức tranh rộng hơn, những chuyển 
biến này chưa gây ra rủi ro quá lớn đến xu hướng đi ngang chủ đạo và chúng tôi kỳ vọng 
VNINDEX sẽ xuất hiện các nhịp hồi phục trở lại, ngay sau khi chớm xuyên thủng các vùng 
đáy ngắn/trung hạn. Yếu tố cần chú ý giai đoạn này là sự phân hóa rõ nét với cơ hội tìm 
kiếm lợi nhuận chỉ tập trung ở nhóm có đủ 2 yếu tố: nền tảng cơ bản tốt và đã về lại các 
vùng hỗ trợ mạnh trên khung lớn.  
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1750 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1670 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.

business demand for labor, and labor supply responded in kind.

4 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.
Exhibit 2: Earnings Growth Resembles a Post-Recession Rebound.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

Investment Implications
We continue to favor staying
invested while using periods of
market weakness to rebalance and
broaden portfolio exposure. As
earnings growth moderates and
market leadership becomes more
differentiated, investors should
remain highly diversified and
emphasize higher-quality exposure.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.

6 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Mercantilist China Powers On: The Relentless Rise of Its Trade Surplus
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
And the boom continues. If investors have learned anything this year, it is that China’s not
ready to adjust its mercantilist growth model. Even in the face of higher U.S. tariffs and a
corresponding decline in exports to the U.S., China recorded a staggering $1T merchandise

trade surplus in just the first 10 months of this year. By the end of 2026, the country’s
trade surplus is on track to surpass 2025’s record and reach something closer to $1.3T3—or
triple the level at the start of the decade ($421 billion4) (Exhibit 4A).
Who is on the receiving end of all these exports? Hint: not the U.S., whose tariffs
taught the rest of the world that it can no longer count on America to absorb a significant
share of China’s exports. Per Exhibit 4B, as recently as 2019, the U.S. accounted for 20% of

exports from China. Today, that share is 10%, down sharply from 15% pre-Liberation Day.
That’s been a bitter pill to swallow for trading partners like the EU and Association of
Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) countries, whose year-to-date deficits with China have
ballooned by 29% and 34% YoY, respectively. Germany, whose local manufacturing industry
continues to struggle under the weight of competition from the mainland, has seen its
deficit with China double in just the last year. It’s no surprise, then, that the chorus of

complaints against China’s mercantilist approach continues to swell beyond Washington.
From quotas on electric vehicle imports in the EU to anti-dumping investigations in India to
product-specific tariffs in Mexico, Brazil, and South Africa, the world is pushing back against
China and its well-subsidized industries.
Looking ahead to 2027, a full-blown tit-for-tat global trade war is far from our base
case. However, we wouldn’t be surprised to see more targeted tariffs, investigations, and

industrial subsidies from developed and emerging markets alike, especially in strategic
sectors like autos/parts, chemicals, steel and basic metals, and other manufactured goods.
In the U.S., despite the Democratic sweep at the midterm elections, tariffs are likely here to
stay via legal measures like Section 232 and Section 301.
For China, more pushback from the rest of the world could force the economy to finally
come to terms with persistently depressed domestic demand. Meanwhile, investment

in domestic manufacturing should continue in the U.S. and Europe. We favor the former
over the latter in portfolios because the diversified nature of the U.S. economy, existing
protectionist measures, and tilt toward services and innovation leave growth relatively less
exposed to competition in manufacturing.
Exhibit 4: Taking Stock of China’s #1 Growth Engine.
A) China On Track for Another Record Annual Trade Surplus. B) Breaking it Down: Who Receives China's Exports?

should be relatively less exposed to

China on U.S. technology supremacy is
an ongoing key risk to watch.

Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to

S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Chicago Fed National Activity Index is a monthly index designed to gauge overall economic activity and related inflationary pressure.
Consumer Price Index is a measure of the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services. Indexes are available for the

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

### Page 5

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

## Document 378: Manus联创张涛：免费补贴是AI应用创业的最大陷阱，要面向“六个月后的模型”做创新

Source PDF: all report pdf/Manus联创张涛：免费补贴是AI应用创业的最大陷阱，要面向“六个月后的模型”做创新.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

张涛在北京⼤学演讲指出， AI 应⽤创业应⾯向 6 个⽉后的模型外溢能⼒设计产品，⽽⾮修补
当下的模型能⼒； 1 秒延迟的视频⽣成模型出现时，可能成为新⼀代操作系统。他还提醒，
免费补贴是 AI 时代最⼤的陷阱，它制造⽆效反馈、扭曲产品判断，让⽤⼾真实感受到智能
才是 AI 产品的价值。 “ 我们被上⼀代移动互联⽹创业的免费补贴陷阱给迷惑了。 ”
摘要：
近⽇， Manus 联合创始⼈、⾸席产品官张涛在北京⼤学 AI 创投课堂发表了⼀场⻓达两⼩时的演
讲，披露了这家 30 ⼈公司从 0 到 1 的创业决策链条。在这场演讲中，张涛抛出了⼀个在当前 AI 创
业圈颇具争议的判断：免费补贴是 AI 应⽤创业最⼤的陷阱，中国 AI 创业者可能在被上⼀代移动互
联⽹的打法所误导。
张涛在演讲中透露， Manus 发布后第⼀个⽉的 ARR （年度经常性收⼊）即突破 4000 万元⼈⺠
币，接近 5000 万，核⼼⽤⼾中不乏每⽉在平台消费 4000 ⾄ 5000 美元的企业管理者。他将这⼀成
绩归结于⼀个核⼼判断：在 AI 时代，让⽤⼾真实感受到智能，才是产品最重要的价值，⽽⾮靠补

贴堆砌 DAU 。
砍掉 7 个⽉⼼⾎：⼀个关于时间成本的战略判断
在 Manus 诞⽣之前，有⼀段鲜为⼈知的弯路。
2024 年 3 ⽉，张涛与联合创始⼈、⾸席科学家 Pick 加⼊肖弘（ Red ）的团队后，团队的第⼀个⽅
向并⾮ Agent ，⽽是⼀款 AI 原⽣浏览器。逻辑顺理成章：公司此前的产品 Monica 是浏览器插件，
天花板明显， " 做浏览器插件出⾝的公司，很容易推演到下⼀个产品应该做个浏览器 " 。
这款浏览器做了整整 7 个⽉，到 2024 年 10 ⽉已接近发布状态。张涛坦⾔， " 发出来应该对市场还
是有点惊艳的 " 。但肖弘最终拍板将其 sunset （放弃）。
我觉得回看过去两三年，中国 AI 应⽤创业，我觉得有⼀个⾮常⼤的陷阱，就是我们被上⼀代移动
互联⽹创业的免费补贴陷阱给迷惑了，以为这⼀代还要这么做，完全错误。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

核⼼原因有两个。其⼀，产品体验的嫁接困境：所有⼈拿到 AI 浏览器后，第⼀个问题都是 " 我在
Chrome ⾥⽤的功能，在你这⾥去哪找 " ，⽤⼾永远⽤旧体验的基准线来衡量新产品，⽽创业团队
根本⽆⼒在浏览器基础功能上对⻬苹果和 Google 。其⼆，时间成本的判断：⼀旦发布，团队将
被迫在这个⽅向上耗费 3 ⾄ 6 个⽉，⽽ " 如果你在创业，钱是最不值钱的东西，浪费了时间才是最
可怕的 " 。
张涛事后评价，这是 Manus 历史上最重要的⼀个决定， " 如果当时不是因为 sunset 了那个浏览器
产品，就没有后来的 Manus" 。
看⻅拐点：从 Cursor ⾥读出的不是 Coding 机会
2024 年 7 ⽉， Cursor 爆⽕。市场上随即涌现出⼤量 Coding Agent 的跟随者。张涛和 Pick 看到的却
是另⼀件事。
" 我们发现 Sonnet 3.5 V2 解锁的不是单纯的 Coding 能⼒，⽽是⼀个 agentic 能⼒。 " 张涛说，这包
括两个维度： Long Horizon Planning （⻓程规划） —— 模型开始主动将任务拆解为多步骤，⽽

⾮在单轮对话中强⾏完成；以及⼤幅增强的 Tool Use （⼯具调⽤）能⼒。
在张涛看来，这个能⼒解锁的对象不是⼯程师，⽽是普通⼈。 " 这个世界不需要更多的 engineer
了，⽽且 engineer 也不需要更多 fancy 的⼯具了。 " 他们看到的机会是：通过 Coding 作为中介，去
解决普通⽤⼾的通⽤场景问题。
2024 年 12 ⽉， Manus 正式⽴项。两个半⽉后， 2025 年 3 ⽉ 5 ⽇发布。发布当天，张涛才发
现， " 好像全世界只有我们在做这个，⼤家都还是在做 Coding" 。
这背后有⼀套 Pick ⼀直坚持的架构哲学，张涛将其概括为 "Less structure, more intelligence" ：
Manus 的 Honeycomb 架构从第⼀版起就完全抛弃了 workflow ，每⼀步的思考和决策全部交由模
型⾃主决定， " 不管你让 Manus 做 research 、做 slides 、做⽹站、写数据分析，所有的事情都是
Manus ⾃⼰决定下⼀步⼲什么的 " 。

这个赌注在 2025 年 2 ⽉中旬 Anthropic 发布 Claude 3.7 的当晚得到验证。张涛在即刻发⽂ " 信仰充
值的⼀晚 "——Honeycomb ⼀⾏代码未改，仅将模型 endpoint 从 3.5 V2 切换⾄ 3.7 ， benchmark 上
剩余未解决的问题 " 百分之⼆三⼗，全部⼀下就解了 " 。
反对 “ 互联⽹式烧钱 ” ：免费补贴是 AI 创业最⼤的陷阱
Manus 发布时，连付费功能都没有做。3 ⽉ 5 ⽇上线，发现⽕爆程度超出预期，团队才从零开始
搭建定价体系，到 3 ⽉ 20 多号才上线付费功能。
⽽在这中间，邀请码成了救命稻草。原因极为现实：公司账上只剩⼋九百万美元，在有邀请码
限制的情况下，单⽇最⾼烧掉 50 万美元。 " 如果没有邀请码，我们公司从 3 ⽉ 5 号发布，可能就⼀
撑⼀个星期， 3 ⽉ 12 号就可以关⻔了。 "
但张涛想说的不只是这段险情，⽽是⼀个更根本的判断：AI 时代，免费补贴是错误⽅向。
我觉得回看过去两三年，中国 AI 应⽤创业有⼀个⾮常⼤的陷阱，就是我们被上⼀代移动互联⽹创

业的免费补贴陷阱给迷惑了，以为这⼀代还要这么做，完全错误。
为什么？因为 AI 这个时代，智能本⾝，如果说你的 performance 本⾝是⾜够好的，⼀定会有⼈
愿意付费的，⼀定是有⼈愿意付费的。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
他的逻辑是：免费补贴吸引来的是⽆效⽤⼾，这些⽤⼾的反馈会迷惑创业者的注意⼒；⽽付费
⽤⼾是⽤钱投票的⽤⼾，他们的需求才是真实需求。 " 你围绕那个需求去做，你就能吸引更多愿
意付费的⼈。 "
他还特别点出⼤⼚的困境：上线第⼀天拿⼀两千万预算补贴⽤⼾，有了⼀⼆⼗万 DAU 之后，没
有⼈敢在第⼆个⽉转向收费， " 它就⼀直拖累了你对于真实⽤⼾市场的感知能⼒ " 。
Manus ⽬前最满意且付费最多的⽤⼾群体，是企业管理者 ——CEO 、⼩⽼板、部⻔主管。张涛
的解释直接： "General AI Agent ， General 通⽤，在公司⾥⾯谁的屁事最多？⽼板嘛。 "
下⼀个转折点在哪⾥：⾯向 “6 个⽉后的模型 ”
张涛在演讲中给出了⼀个具体的创业⽅向预判，他称之为 "1 秒钟延迟的 Sora 时刻 " 。
" 我⼀直在等待⼀个时刻，就是从你输出 prompt 到你看到画⾯延迟⼩于 1 秒钟。我觉得当这个
turning point 到来的时候，会是这⼀轮 AI 整个应⽤创业⾮常重要的转折点。 "

他的判断是， 1 秒延迟的视频⽣成模型已经不是视频⽣成模型，" 它就是新⼀代的操作系统 "。届
时的交互⽅式将从单向内容消费变为每个⼈⾃主参与的互动创作， " 它已经不是单纯的⼀⼈制
作、多⼈消费了 " 。他认为这⼀时刻 "6 个⽉之内⼀定会发⽣ " ，并⿎励创业者现在就开始围绕这个
⽅向准备产品形态。
这⼀判断背后，是张涛对 AI 创业⽅法论的⼀贯主张：永远⾯向 6 个⽉后的模型外溢能⼒去创新，
⽽不是基于今天的模型能⼒修修补补。 " 你应该想象的是 6 个⽉之后模型会变成什么样⼦。如果 6
个⽉之后模型是那个样⼦，我会⽤什么样的产品形态去匹配那个时候的模型。 "
他同时强调，坚持做 General （通⽤）是 Manus 的另⼀个关键战略判断。 " 品牌⼼智的认知是很
难花钱去买的，你在⽤⼾⼼中的 Mindset ，是花⼏亿美⾦都很难改变的事情。 " 正因为⼀开始占住
了 " 什么都能⼲ " 的⼼智， Manus 在后续推出 WebDev 、 Slides 、 Video 等⼦功能时都顺理成章，⽽

那些从垂直场景起家的竞品，想转向通⽤任务时则⾯临⽤⼾认知的强烈阻⼒。
在资本效率问题上，张涛透露， Manus 做到 20 亿美元估值时，历史上总共花掉的融资额约为
5000 ⾄ 6000 万美元。 " 我们团队可能是国内 AI startup ⾥资本利⽤效率最⾼的⼀⽀团队。 " 他的结
论是： AI 创业与其他创业没有本质区别， " 不是因为加了个 AI 的定语，你就可以很理所当然地亏
钱 " 。

### Page 4

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

mid-cycle market dynamics. Last week, as the US equity 
market volatility index VIX surged above 25, we partially dialled 
up risk with a tactical idea on the more diversified US IT sector. 
Staying tactical within a diversified allocation. Equity 
volatility following last week’s Democratic clean sweep in the 
US elections has subsided. Questions about the pace of AI data 
centre buildout are likely to linger until Democrats take office in 
in January. Nvidia’s turmoil adds uncertainty. We remain 
broadly diversified and balanced in our core allocation across 
assets, sector and regions, while seeking tactical opportunities. 
History suggests sharp selloffs are followed by near-term 
volatility, particularly during macroeconomic stress. However, 
the balance of historical outcomes has typically shifted strongly 
to the upside over a three- to six-month horizon. Besides, 
historically, US equity markets have delivered the strongest 12-
month returns after opposition clean sweeps in the mid-terms. 
Rotation driving US IT sector recovery: As volatility eases, 
our opportunistic US IT equities idea, launched a week ago, has 
performed well, despite some significantly negative company 
specific developments. Attractive valuations support further 
upside. Investors are rotating within IT from semiconductors, 
affected by concerns over the pace of AI data centre buildout 
and Nvidia’s troubles, towards software and hardware 
providers. The latter have strong cash flows and balance sheets 
and are likely to benefit from AI monetisation. Together, they 
account for more than 60% of the IT sector (see page 4).

Remain constructive on US healthcare. US healthcare 
earnings are projected to grow almost 23% in 2027, the second-
fastest among MSCI US sectors. We see scope for further 
upside in the sector as innovation underpins long-term earnings 
growth. With the market rotating away from concentrated AI 
exposure, healthcare's stable, non-cyclical demand profile 
offers a compelling exposure as a diversifier (see page 5). 
Outside the US, Switzerland and India offer diversified 
returns. The Swiss equity market consists of 41% healthcare, 
19% financial services and 14% consumer staples, providing a 
defensive alternative to investors seeking to diversify away 
from tech-heavy exposure. The Indian equity market has been 
classified as an “anti-AI” trade. Historically narrow valuation 
premiums vs. EMs, low foreign investor positioning and c. 15% 
expected 2027 earnings growth make it an attractive diversifier. 
Strong jobs and inflation reports cement December Fed 
rate hike. The US economy added 165,000 jobs in October, 
more than twice the 70,000 consensus estimate. The 
unemployment rate fell to 4.1%. Meanwhile, headline and core 
inflation in October, at 0.4% and 0.3% m/m, respectively, 
exceeded expectations as elevated fuel prices, ‘techflation’ and 
earlier tariffs fed into directly exposed sectors, including food, 
utilities and transportation services. A healthy job market keeps 
the Fed focused on lowering inflation, cementing the prospect 
of a December rate hike, potentially followed by another in Q1 
2027. A strong economy and elevated interest rates support our 
overweight allocation to US financial sector equities. 
Locking in gains from our bullish US inflation-protected 
bonds idea. While near-term price pressures remain elevated, 
we expect inflation to ease with energy costs over the coming 
year. Republicans’ heavy losses in the US mid-term elections 
increase pressure on the Trump administration to end the Iran 
conflict. One-year US inflation expectations have slumped 
alongside oil prices. A potential slowdown in the pace of AI 
buildout is also likely to ease techflation. Meanwhile, Fed rate 
hikes should further tighten financial conditions, tempering 
price pressures. This disinflationary outlook makes the risk-
reward for US inflation-protected bonds less compelling. We 
close the idea, locking in a 4.4% gain (page 6). 
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

What does history tell us about the aftermath of a single-
day sell-off of 4% or more in the S&P500 index?

Our view: The S&P500 fell 4.36% on 5 November 2026, a rare daily 
move of 3.8 standard deviations. Since 2009, there have been only

24 occasions when the index declined by 4% or more in a single

session. The immediate aftermath of such sell-offs has historically

been mixed. One-month returns ranged from around -15% to

+23%, with positive returns in 70% of cases. The weaker

outcomes were concentrated around periods of significant macro

stress, including the tail end of the 2008 Global Financial Crisis

(GFC), the 2020 COVID-19 pandemic and the 2022 monetary policy

tightening cycle. 
The historical picture becomes considerably more constructive

beyond the first month. All 24 episodes generated positive three-
month returns, ranging from roughly +0.5% to +38%, while six-
month returns were positive in 96% of cases, with gains reaching 
as much as +49%. Overall, history suggests sharp sell-offs can be 
followed by near-term volatility, particularly during periods of broader 
macro stress. However, the balance of historical outcomes has 
shifted strongly to the upside over a 3-6-month horizon. 
—  Maggie Au Yeung, Quantitative Analyst 
 o
— Francis Lim, Senior Quantitative Strategist
Where do you see the most favourable risk-reward within

the US technology sector? 
Our view: We favour high-quality Big Tech companies with 
strong balance sheets and prefer diversified exposure through 
our US Information Technology (IT) opportunistic idea. 
Rationale: The recent pullback in US semiconductor stocks has 
improved their risk-reward profile. Earnings for these companies are 
forecast to grow by 65% in 2027, the fastest rate among technology 
subsectors. Despite this outlook, recent regulatory and corporate 
governance concerns are weighing on sentiment. Potential state-
level restrictions on data centre development following the recent 
US mid-term election results could weigh on semiconductor demand 
growth. However, we believe these risks are manageable and 
continued AI adoption should support demand for AI inference. We 
prefer diversified exposure to quality semiconductor 
companies with durable demand and strong volume growth. 
To navigate volatility in the US technology sector, we favour Big Tech 
companies with strong balance sheets and resilient cash generation, 
as they are better positioned to sustain AI investments without 
relying heavily on external financing. Our US IT opportunistic idea 
combines these preferences through exposure to semiconductors, 
software and hardware, allowing investors to participate in a 
potential recovery across a broader range of earnings drivers.
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d)

Do fundamental drivers support a constructive view on the 
US healthcare sector?

Our view: We remain constructive on US healthcare, supported

by its defensive characteristics and robust earnings growth.

Rationale: US healthcare earnings are projected to grow by 22.5% 
in 2027, the second-fastest among MSCI US sectors, driven by 
improving biopharma earnings. The US healthcare sector's 
valuation gap against the broader market has been recovering from 
a steep historical discount a year ago. The sector's forward price-to-

earnings (P/E) ratio of c. 20x now exceeds the broader US market’s

c. 18.4x, but we see scope for the valuation to be supported amid

investor demand for defensive growth. The sector’s broad legislative

policy overhang has materially faded following the US election cycle,

while targeted regulatory and oversight risks remain on the watchlist.

Healthcare innovation also underpins structural growth. Wider

adoption of obesity and diabetes treatments, alongside advances in

oncology treatments, medical devices and robotic surgery, should

support long-term earnings growth. With the market rotating away

from concentrated AI exposure, healthcare's stable, non-cyclical 
demand profile presents compelling opportunities for investors 
seeking both defensiveness and growth in the near term.
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst
What is your outlook for China’s Big Tech sector? 
Our view: We remain positive on China’s Big Tech sector, 
consistent with our Overweight stance on the country’s IT and 
communication services sector. 
Rationale: Valuations across China’s technology sector remain 
inexpensive at 14.2x, with investor attention centred on whether AI-
driven revenue can offset elevated AI-related spending, which is 
currently compressing margins. Elevated AI capex and negative 
free cash flow remain key investor concerns in the Q3 earnings 
season. Top-line momentum also remains uneven: soft domestic 
consumption continues to weigh on e-commerce, advertising and 
local services, whereas gaming and cloud computing are 
delivering resilient growth. The broader narrative is steadily 
transitioning towards commercialisation, with rapid enterprise 
penetration by AI workplace agents and upcoming AI model 
launches shifting the market focus towards active deployment and 
conversion to paid usage. Evolving regulatory frameworks 
covering AI governance, agent self-regulation, tax scrutiny and 
compliance requirements remain important variables influencing 
medium-term margin trajectories. Ultimately, valuation support, 
gradual monetisation of AI capabilities and resilient core 
segments support our Overweight positioning.
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist
US healthcare sector earnings are projected to 
grow 22.5% in 2027 – the second-fastest among 
MSCI US sectors

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

Why are you closing your US Treasury Inflation-Protected

Securities (TIPS) opportunistic idea? 
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish US TIPS 
position, as the risk-reward profile has become less compelling.

The trade returned 4.4% since its initiation on 11 April 2025.

Rationale: We initiated an opportunistic bullish position in US TIPS

on 11 April 2025 as an oil and inflation hedge, expecting geopolitical

uncertainty to keep inflation expectations elevated. The risk-reward,

however, has since become less compelling, as hawkish Fed 
commentary, easing geopolitical risks and a potential slowdown in 
the pace of AI buildout are now likely to ease broader inflation and 
‘techflation’ pressures. We have therefore closed the opportunistic

idea, realising a gain of 4.4%.
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist
How do falling oil prices and October’s US inflation data 
affect your Fed and bond market outlook? 
Our view: Our base case envisages two further 25bps Fed hikes 
by mid-2027, with scope for cuts in H2 2027 if energy prices 
remain consistently lower and growth trajectory normalises. We 
favour income over chasing duration gains, focusing on higher-
quality corporate bonds with 3-7-year maturities. 
Rationale: Lower oil prices have a two-sided effect: they ease 
inflationary pressures and support growth by boosting purchasing 
power and lowering business costs. Cheaper energy, therefore, 
does not automatically justify Fed easing. Our base case envisages 
two further 25bps hikes by mid-2027, with scope for cuts in H2 
2027 if energy prices remain consistently lower. Sustained 
moderation in core inflation would challenge the near-term tightening 
path. However, October’s US consumer inflation print reinforced our 
assessment that each Fed meeting now remains a ‘live’ decision. 
With the US 10-year government bond yield already within our 5.0-
5.25% 3-month range, we favour income over chasing duration 
gains. Credit performance has diverged: Developed Market high-
yield bonds have outperformed both investment-grade credit and 
government bonds. Credit still offers attractive income, and we retain 
our preference for 3-7-year maturities, although heavy primary 
issuance remains a counterweight. We favour selective carry, with a 
preference for higher-quality corporate bonds. These include bonds 
issued by leading AI hyperscalers, where risk-reward has become 
more attractive. Our emphasis is on generating steady income, 
rather than relying on further declines in bond yields or spreads.
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist
While real (net-of-inflation) yields remain elevated, 
the inflation-protection function of US TIPS has 
diminished as inflation expectations ease

Standard Chartered Bank

WS Global CIO Office | 13 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d)
Does the USD’s rally have further to run?

Our view: Further upside appears limited. Stronger-than-

expected US core inflation in October and hawkish Fed

comments have pushed the US Dollar Index (DXY) above 103.

However, markets already price in more Fed tightening than we

expect. We see the DXY returning to its 101.00-102.95 range in

the near term, before gradually weakening towards our 12-

month forecast of 98.5.

Rationale: US core consumer prices rose 0.3% m/m in October,

above the 0.2% m/m expected, meeting Fed Governor Waller’s

threshold for considering a 50bps December rate hike. This pushed

US government bond yields higher, supporting the USD rally. 
However, futures now imply a Fed rate peak of 4.50-4.75% by

mid-2027, compared with our expectation of 4.25-4.50% following

two 25bps hikes, one in December and the second in H1 2027. We

expect the Fed to begin easing in H2 2027. As market expectations 
gradually converge towards our view, USD support from US bond 
yields should weaken.
Meanwhile, falling oil and petrol prices should help ease headline 
inflation in the coming months, reducing pressure for further Fed 
tightening. This reinforces our expectation of limited USD upside, 
although persistent services inflation remains a key risk.
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist
Do you expect USD/JPY to sustainably break out above 
160 in the near term?
Our view: We see USD/JPY rangebound with a downside bias, 
as growing BoJ tightening expectations and potential policy 
convergence will support gradual JPY appreciation.
Rationale: Renewed tech optimism has encouraged the re-
establishment of carry trades, providing support to the pair lately. 
Meanwhile, US core inflation beat expectations for a third 
consecutive month in October, reinforcing expectations for a 
December Fed hike. Further upside inflation surprises could widen 
the US-Japan interest rate differential and push USD/JPY higher. 
However, the risk of intervention by Japan’s Ministry of Finance 
is likely to rise materially around 160, limiting the scope for 
sustained upside. On the downside, a 25bps BoJ hike to 1.50%, 
accompanied by clear signals of consecutive hikes in March and 
June 2027, would be a significant yen-positive catalyst. A more non-
linear downside scenario would be a disorderly equity sell-off, which 
could trigger rapid JPY appreciation through carry-trade unwinding. 
We see scope for the pair to test support towards 156 in the 
coming weeks.
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist
US dollar index’s rally above 103 looks stretched

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM) (5 
November)

As written in our Macro Strategy note, 
BNM’s final meeting of 2026 on 5 
November will be live and a close call, 
after the central bank opened the door to 
monetary policy normalisation during its 
September review. BNM Governor Abdul 
Rasheed Ghaffour reiterated in a 
Bloomberg interview on 15 October that 
the central bank has space to adjust 
policy if needed, as policymakers are 
vigilant about upside inflationary 
pressures amid strong economic growth. 
He also said that policy decisions will be 
data dependent. We think the latest 
available September data for the 
upcoming meeting suggest that 
policymakers can afford to be patient 
until 1H27, maintaining the Overnight 
Policy Rate at 2.75% for now. Inflation 
remained contained within the lower half 
of the official 2026 inflation forecast 
range of 1.5-2.5%, notwithstanding 
external price pressures and robust 
economic activity. Advance estimates 
showed GDP growth of 5.3% yoy in 3Q26, 
while exports jumped to a five-year high 
of 54.5% yoy in September, 
supercharged by the artificial 
intelligence boom. However, headline 
inflation eased slightly to 1.8% yoy in 
September 2026 from 1.9% yoy in August, 
while core inflation remained stable at 1.7% 
yoy. Fuel subsidies have limited the pass-
through of upstream cost pressures to 
consumer prices, alongside stable 
domestic demand conditions.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

South Korea

October trade and inflation data are due 
for release. Export growth is expected to 
accelerate to nearly 100% yoy, up from 
83.7% in September, while the trade 
surplus is forecast to remain above 
USD40bn for another month. CPI 
inflation is expected to hold steady at 2.9% 
yoy. Overall, the data should reinforce 
the view that AI- and semiconductor-
driven growth and the expansion in 
South Korea’s trade surplus remain 
firmly on track, providing fundamental 
support for the KOSPI and KRW. 
Meanwhile, inflation remains sticky 
above the BOK’s 2% target, bolstering 
the case for further monetary tightening 
as early as the November policy meeting.

Taiwan

October inflation data are forthcoming. 
Headline CPI inflation is expected to 
remain above 2% for the sixth 
consecutive month, at 2.3% yoy. Core 
CPI inflation is also likely to stay above 2% 
for the sixth consecutive month, at 2.3% 
yoy. Despite government subsidies on 
fuel and electricity prices, higher global 
energy prices continue to feed through 
to CPI via items such as airfares and tour 
package fees. Services inflation also 
remains sticky, particularly in food away 
from home, housing, transportation, and 
entertainment. Meanwhile, domestic 
inflation expectations appear to be 
gradually rising. A recent private-sector 
survey showed that the public expects

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

inflation to average 2.5% in 2026, with 
52% of respondents expecting inflation 
to exceed 2.5%. Against this backdrop, 
we maintain our view that the central 
bank will resume rate hikes in December, 
raising the policy rate by 12.5 bps to 
2.125%.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to rise to 3.2% yoy in October 2026, 
extending the increase over the previous 
two months, and moving slightly above 
the upper end of the Bank of Thailand’s 
1-3% target range. This was likely driven 
by faster energy inflation amid an 
uptrend in diesel prices, alongside a 
rebound in food inflation. The inflation 
uptrend was likely broadly in line with 
the central bank's expectations and 
mainly supply-side driven, supporting its 
decision to look through the temporary 
price pressures and keep its policy rate 
unchanged at its October meeting.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s October 2026 
goods exports and retail sales to remain 
robust at 42.0% yoy and 14.0% yoy year-
to-date, respectively, while headline 
inflation is likely to stay elevated at 
around 5.0% yoy. Exports have 
continued to be supported by strong 
electronics and machinery shipments 
amid a favourable technology cycle with 
spillovers from robust artificial 
intelligence-related demand. Retail 
sales were likely bolstered by firm 
household spending, supported by 
favourable labour market conditions, 
and continued strength in tourism. 
Headline inflation has remained elevated 
and above the central bank’s target of 
4.5% for the year, driven by price 
pressures from transport and energy, 
food, as well as material costs. This 
poses a challenge for policymakers as 
they seek to balance inflation risks 
against ambitions of achieving at least 10% 
economic growth this year.

Hong Kong

Hong Kong’s economy is expected to 
decelerate from 4.3% yoy in Q2 to 2.6% 
in Q3, largely due to slower private 
consumption. Despite a 13% increase in 
Mainland visitor arrivals during the 
period, domestic consumption continued 
to be weighed down by a weakening 
labour market, with the unemployment 
rate elevated at 3.9% in September. 
Meanwhile, higher interest rates have 
dampened investment sentiment and 
capital market activity. The Centa-City 
Leading Index remained broadly flat, 
while the Hang Seng Index was range-
bound at around 25,000. The positive 
wealth effect seen in 1H has faded as 
asset market performance remained 
muted. A strong HKD continued to 
encourage outbound tourism, evidenced 
by an increase in average daily resident 
departures from 6.9% yoy in Q2 to 10.9% 
in Q3.

However, GDP growth is expected to be 
buffered by improving net exports amid 
the ongoing AI-electronics upcycle. 
Export growth picked up and outpaced 
import growth during Q3. Hong Kong, as 
China’s free port and re-export hub, is 
benefiting from stronger demand for 
Chinese electronic goods.

Indonesia/ Philippines

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 30, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Indonesia’s 3Q GDP likely moderated to 
5.2% y/y. Soft consumer confidence and 
retail sales growth point to weaker 
consumption, compounded by a gradual 
rise in inflation. Fiscal measures likely 
helped spending among low-income 
households. Despite the quarterly 
moderation, we maintain our full-year 
growth forecast at 5.3% y/y.

Inflation rates in Indonesia and the 
Philippines are expected to rise, albeit to 
different degrees. Indonesia’s inflation is 
on course to edge up modestly, while in 
the Philippines, stronger pass-through 
from higher oil prices, food and 
transportation costs, as well as imported 
inflation pressures stemming from a 
weaker peso, is likely to push inflation to 
a multi-year high.

## Document 428: Triển vọng kinh tế Việt Nam 4Q2026 (2026-10-19)

Trung tâm phân tích 
(+84) 24-7303-5333  kbsv.research@kbsec.com.vn

19/10/2026

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về đích

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tổng quan kinh tế vĩ mô Việt Nam

Quý III/2026, tổng sản phẩm trong nước (GDP) tăng 9.95% YoY, cao hơn mức 
tăng 8.81% của quý II, đưa tăng trưởng 9T2026 lên 9.01%, với động lực tiếp 
tục đến từ hoạt động đầu tư và sản xuất công nghiệp - xây dựng. Mức tăng này 
cho thấy hoạt động kinh tế về tổng thể vẫn duy trì đà tăng tích cực trong bối 
cảnh môi trường quốc tế còn nhiều bất định. Tuy nhiên, để đạt mục tiêu tăng 
trưởng hai con số trong năm 2026, GDP quý IV cần tăng khoảng 12.5% YoY 
(theo ước tính của Chính phủ), cho thấy áp lực trong quý cuối năm là rất lớn.

Chúng tôi dự báo tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 đạt khoảng 9.0%, với động lực 
chính đến từ định hướng tiếp tục đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính 
phủ, dòng vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo xuất 
khẩu. Rủi ro trong quý IV gồm rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu 
hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo 
giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

Tổng sản phẩm trong nước GDP

GDP 3Q2026 – Tăng trưởng ấn tượng 
Tăng trưởng GDP quý III tích cực 
bất chấp những rủi ro bên ngoài

Quý III/2026, tổng sản phẩm trong nước (GDP) tăng 9.95% YoY – mức cao nhất 
cùng kỳ trong bốn năm trở lại đây, đưa tăng trưởng 9T2026 lên 9.01%, với động 
lực tăng trưởng tiếp tục đến từ hoạt động đầu tư và hoạt động sản xuất công 
nghiệp - xây dựng.

Xét từ phía cầu, đầu tư tiếp tục là động lực quan trọng của tăng trưởng, được 
hỗ trợ bởi tiến độ giải ngân đầu tư công và dòng vốn FDI duy trì tích cực; trong 
khi tiêu dùng trong nước đã có sự phục hồi trở lại, cụ thể: 
(1) Tổng vốn đầu tư thực hiện toàn xã hội quý III/2026 đạt 1,291 nghìn tỷ 
đồng, tăng 16.7% YoY. Lũy kế 9T2026 đạt 3,110 nghìn tỷ đồng, tăng 
15.1% YoY, trong đó: (i) vốn khu vực FDI đạt 529.4 nghìn tỷ đồng 
(+14.5% YoY) với số vốn FDI giải ngân lũy kế cao nhất trong 5 năm qua 
(đạt 21.07 tỷ USD, +12.1% YoY); (ii) vốn khu vực nhà nước đạt 931 nghìn 
tỷ, vốn giải ngân đầu tư công đạt 643 nghìn tỷ đồng – tăng mạnh 45.5% 
so với cùng kỳ, đạt 64.8 % kế hoạch Thủ tướng Chính phủ giao. 
(2) Tổng mức bán lẻ hàng hóa và doanh thu dịch vụ tiêu dùng quý III/2026 
ước đạt 2,039 nghìn tỷ đồng (+14.5% YoY). Lũy kế 9T2026, tổng doanh 
thu theo giá hiện hành tăng 13.4% YoY; nếu loại trừ yếu tố giá, mức 
tăng đạt 7.8% (cao hơn mức 7.2% cùng kỳ), nhờ nhu cầu chi tiêu dịch 
vụ lưu trú, ăn uống và du lịch nội địa có sự cải thiện.

Xét từ phía cung, cả ba khu vực kinh tế đều duy trì đà tăng trưởng, trong đó 
công nghiệp – xây dựng là trụ cột chính, cụ thể: 
(1) Khu vực công nghiệp và xây dựng dẫn đầu với mức tăng trưởng 12.5% 
YoY trong quý III/2026; lũy kế 9T2026 tăng 11.21% YoY, đóng góp 
49.62% vào tăng trưởng chung — phản ánh đà phục hồi mạnh mẽ từ 
hoạt động sản xuất của khu vực FDI và hoạt động thi công hạ tầng 
trong nước.  
(2) Khu vực dịch vụ quý III/2026 tăng 9.54% YoY; lũy kế 9T2026 tăng 8.69% 
YoY, đóng góp 45.03% vào tăng trưởng chung của toàn nền kinh tế. 
(3) Khu vực nông, lâm nghiệp và thủy sản quý III/2026 tăng 4.21% YoY; lũy 
kế 9T2026 tăng 4.02%, đóng góp 5.35% vào mức tăng tổng giá trị tăng 
thêm của toàn nền kinh tế.
